- [x] ✅ A, 08-18: FIXED — the design call is 'my recorded picks bound the clock below' (currentPick now returns max(room clock, mine[k-1]+1) in manual mode; sync untouched). Reproduced-then-green in rehearsal-mock3's new FALLBACK CLOCK scenario, 20/20. En route: endDraft now re-seeds keepers (they are placements, not picks). · 2026-08-17 · B · 🔴🔴 **FOUND DURING THE ITEM-2 REHEARSAL, AND IT IS SERIOUS: THE DRAFT-NIGHT FALLBACK CLOCK BREAKS ON YOUR VERY FIRST PICK IN MANUAL MODE.** Not inferred — reproduced step by step through the actual UI (`.wrv-take`, real clicks, real console), not a blind script loop; I checked the naive version of this test first, found it was clicking a stale reference, and rebuilt it before trusting anything.
  **THE SYMPTOM, verified live:** in manual/rehearsal mode (no Sleeper sync — the exact draft-night fallback path, per `app.js`'s own comment: *"Manual mode is the DRAFT-NIGHT FALLBACK, so this was the one path that had to work when sync failed"*), taking my first pick fires `console.error('[pick-state] [roster marked] 1 picks != [my_picks < clock] 0 made — the need model is reading MORE picks than the clock')` — and it repeats, unchanged, on every subsequent pick (verified through 3 real picks, plus 9 opponent picks marked `Gone` in between, which does not clear it either). **It is also visibly wrong on screen, not just in the console:** the WATCH strip's `Starters: N/9` correctly advances (3/9 → 4/9 → 5/9 as I took real players) while `12 picks left` on the SAME line never moves, through all three picks. Two numbers on one banner, one updating correctly and one frozen — the exact class of contradiction this whole project has been hunting for weeks.
  **THE MECHANISM, traced to source:** `currentPick()` (`app.js:1669`) has special-case pre-draft handling (`if (ps.pickEvents === 0) return mine[0]` — correctly anchors to my real first pick, e.g. 33, before anything is recorded) but the moment `pickEvents` becomes non-zero — i.e., the instant I take my FIRST pick — it falls through to `ps.currentPick`, which `pickState()` (`app.js:1511`) defines as `pickEvents + 1`. That treats the count of MY locally-recorded picks as if it were the room's absolute pick number, discarding the 32 picks that happened before my seat's turn. So after pick 1, `currentPick` becomes `1 + 1 = 2` instead of continuing from 33 — and `myPicksLeft()`/`accounting.js`'s `picksMade` (`myPicks.filter(p => p < currentPick)`) then compares my real pick numbers (33, 48, 68…) against a clock stuck near 2, so **`picksMade` reads 0 forever, no matter how many picks you actually take.** Confirmed this is manual-only: `state.sync` present uses `state.sync.currentPickNumber() - 1`, the real room position, and does not have this bug — it's specific to the unsynced fallback.
  **WHY IT MATTERS BEYOND THE ONE VISIBLE LINE:** `myPicksLeft()` feeds `DraftLegality.assess` (`app.js:7229`/`7544`), and the same `picksMade`/`picksLeft` pair feeds the `mustDraftNow`/`onesieSqueeze` endgame logic in `legality.js`. If the clock never advances past ~2, every one of those calculations is running on a permanently-wrong "how many picks do I have left" — which is exactly the number that decides when the tool tells you to stop waiting on a onesie and draft it now. **This isn't cosmetic; it can change what the tool tells you to do, specifically in the mode that exists for when the real connection fails on draft night.**
  **ASK:** this needs `app.js`, which is on hold tonight for the stack/handcuff merge — flagging now rather than waiting, given what it's the fallback FOR. **DEFAULT:** none from me; not proposing a fix since I don't know whether the intended contract is "pickEvents counts from my anchor pick" or something else — that's a design call in your file, not mine to guess at.

- [ ] 2026-08-16 · pace-of-play study (A-lane) · 🏁 **PACE IS A PUBLISHED NULL — NOTHING SHIPS, NO DECISION NEEDED, NO DIFF PREPARED.** Cory asked whether we had studied pace. We had not: `own_model_v5.py`'s `pace_lam` is regression-to-the-mean on prior-season team target/rush VOLUME, inert at QB and RB and a no-op at WR (λ=1.00); `own_model_v6.py` contains the string zero times. Now measured from real nflverse play-by-play. **The shape of the finding, which is more useful than "null":** what everybody MEANS by pace — plays per game — **does not carry year to year** (+0.062 [−0.154, +0.265]); what does carry is **tempo, seconds per play** (+0.417 [+0.191, +0.602], more persistent than the week-1 implied team total at +0.302); tempo **is** orthogonal to implied total (−0.140 [−0.358, +0.077]), so the premise held; and it **still** does not pay because `tempo(Y−1) → plays(Y)` cannot be resolved from zero (−0.181 [−0.368, +0.014]) — measured without reading one fantasy point. Graded anyway on `own_v6`'s own cells: bar not cleared, and the **negative control beats `own_v6` at WR on both metrics** while the registered sign does not, which voids the arm there. **And it is not a "too small to see" null** — the tilt moves CeeDee Lamb +7.1, Rachaad White −10.0 and reorders 49/60 WRs in the draftable range while buying nothing. Verdict `draft/audit/pace_of_play_2026-08-16.md`; prereg + two amendments committed before each step they govern. **Kept for later:** `team_pace_2021_2025.json` (team-season AND team-week, 32/32 measured every season) is there if a weekly arm or in-season usage tool ever wants tempo. **Nothing in `own_model_v*`, `build.py`, `own_projections.py`, `vorp.py`, `projections.py`, `fetch_component_stats.py` or any workflow was touched.**

Now measured from real nflverse play-by-play. **The shape of the finding, which is more useful than "null":** what everybody MEANS by pace — plays per game — **does not carry year to year** (+0.062 [−0.154, +0.265]); what does carry is **tempo, seconds per play** (+0.417 [+0.191, +0.602], more persistent than the week-1 implied team total at +0.302); tempo **is** orthogonal to implied total (−0.140 [−0.358, +0.077]), so the premise held; and it **still** does not pay because `tempo(Y−1) → plays(Y)` cannot be resolved from zero (−0.181 [−0.368, +0.014]) — measured without reading one fantasy point. Graded anyway on `own_v6`'s own cells: bar not cleared, and the **negative control beats `own_v6` at WR on both metrics** while the registered sign does not, which voids the arm there. **And it is not a "too small to see" null** — the tilt moves CeeDee Lamb +7.1, Rachaad White −10.0 and reorders 49/60 WRs in the draftable range while buying nothing. Verdict `draft/audit/pace_of_play_2026-08-16.md`; prereg + two amendments committed before each step they govern. **Kept for later:** `team_pace_2021_2025.json` (team-season AND team-week, 32/32 measured every season) is there if a weekly arm or in-season usage tool ever wants tempo. **Nothing in `own_model_v*`, `build.py`, `own_projections.py`, `vorp.py`, `projections.py`, `fetch_component_stats.py` or any workflow was touched.** 📄 **CLOSED 2026-08-17 BY THE RELAY AS INFORMATIONAL — register 4g.** This item DECLARES ITSELF as requiring no decision, and it was checked for a buried ask before closing (`ASK:` / `THE ONE DECISION` / `MERGE-READY` / `DECISIONS-NEEDED` / *your call*) — **none found.** Two sibling items that read the same way DID carry a buried ask and were deliberately left open, so this is not a headline sweep. **Nothing is deleted:** closed items move to `ROUTES-ARCHIVE.md`. **A gatekeeper's queue should contain decisions; this is a report.** Reopen it if that judgement is wrong.

The tie is inherited: the raw layer-1 model underflows to s=0 for everyone with ADP well inside the window, the tilt is order-preserving on distinct w but maps the w=1 plateau to one value — ordering at the top of the board is already destroyed upstream and the tilt makes it visible at 42 instead of ~0. The 73% Cory saw beside it is a DIFFERENT model: `threatBoard`'s seat-by-seat alive-chain conserves by construction and keeps per-player ordering — two models disagreeing on one screen. Not touched (survival maths = scoring surface, and the chips are the design agent's pass); the fix direction is a modelling decision — either keep raw ordering under the plateau (tilt the hazard, not the probability, e.g. λ·(−ln s) instead of λ·(1−s)) or render the saturated set as ">X% gone" instead of a falsely-uniform point estimate.   **NOT TOUCHED — both files carry `# TERRITORY: A`.** Reproduce with `TIERED_STORE_DIR=<dir> python -c "import tiered_outcome_model as T; print(T.verify_derivation((2023,2024,2025)))"`. - [ ] 2026-08-16 · C · 🕰️ **`own_model_v2.board_ages()` LEAKS THE FUTURE, MILDLY, AND IT WILL LEAK MORE THE FURTHER BACK ANY BACKTEST REACHES.** Recorded rather than acted on; it is your file and the current exposure is small.
  It reads `age` from `public/draft_data.json` — the **2026** board — and hands it to fits graded on earlier seasons. The value is one year stale per season, which is fixable arithmetic. **The part that is not arithmetic is MEMBERSHIP: a player who retired after the graded season is simply absent from the 2026 board, so "has an age at all" is information from after the season being graded.** With v6 graded on 2025 that is one year of survivorship and the effect is small. It is the reason the tiered outcome model **excludes age entirely** rather than using it with a missing-indicator — at a 2023 test season the missingness pattern is a straightforward leak, and an indicator would have made it a feature.
  **Nothing to do before the 22nd.** Flagging it so that if the projection programme gains 2023/2024 as graded seasons (the DECISIONS-NEEDED item filed today would allow exactly that), the age term does not inherit the leak silently.

- [ ] 2026-08-14 · C · 🔴 **`integrate.sh`'s ROUTES MERGE CANNOT EXPRESS A CLOSE, AND IT IS SILENTLY UNDOING EVERYONE'S. `git merge-file --union` keeps every line from BOTH sides — a DELETION has no representation in it, and in this channel the deletion IS the receipt.** Measured today: I closed **fourteen** items in `b809534`, pushed it to main, and two merges later **all fourteen were back in my inbox**. Not a race, not anyone's mistake — it is what `--union` means. **It is also the explanation for the symptom you already recorded from the other side:** `lane-start.sh` reporting nineteen items to B where ten existed. Your per-lane counter was reading a real number produced by this.
  **UNION IS STILL HALF-RIGHT AND MUST NOT BE REVERTED.** Both sides APPEND, so ours-vs-theirs discards one side's new items — the incident union was added for, and it did fix it. The bug is that appends and deletions need OPPOSITE treatment and one flag was applied to both.
  **THE CORRECT SEMANTICS, at the ITEM level rather than the line level:** `result = (mine ∪ theirs) − (base − mine) − (base − theirs)`. Keep everything either side has, then remove anything either side DELETED relative to the merge base. An append survives (in one side, not in base); a close survives (in base, gone from one side). Both sides' work is preserved, which is what union was reaching for.
  **BUILT AND TESTED, yours to adopt: `scripts/routes-merge.py` + `scripts/routes-merge.test.sh` (13 checks).** Drop-in for the union block: `python3 scripts/routes-merge.py BASE MINE THEIRS OUT`. **Both incidents are FAIL ARMS** — the close that union undoes, and the append that ours-vs-theirs drops — plus a CONTROL asserting that a union really does resurrect the closed item, so the difference between the two is asserted rather than described. Also controls for an unchanged merge being a no-op and for both sides closing the same item.
  **AND IT IS SECTION-AWARE, because my first version was not and it reintroduced YOUR incident 2.** Merging one flat item list put an item addressed to A underneath `

  **THE ONE DESIGN DECISION THAT IS EXPENSIVE TO GET WRONG, AND IT IS CHEAP TODAY: THE TERMS MUST BE MACHINE-READABLE AT CREATION TIME.** If a bet is stored as free text — *"I will take the over on Bijan rushing yards"* — then settlement is manual FOREVER, and Cory adjudicate-when-it-can is unbuildable no matter how good the UI is. A bet needs a typed shape: subject (player / team / manager / league), metric, comparator, threshold, resolution window, and the data source that settles it. **The prose sentence should be GENERATED FROM the structured terms for display, never the other way round** — which is also how the gold read-back line generalises to every bet type instead of just the pool. So the record needs pick ORDER and pick SEQUENCE, not just two sets of five. Without the order the bet is still settleable, but the pick-by-pick history — the interesting data — is gone.

- [x] 2026-08-13 · A · 🎲 ✅ (B, 08-17: applied, see the trashtalk item above) **THE TRASHTALK ORDER BUG IS DIAGNOSED, FIXED AND VERIFIED — AS A PATCH, BECAUSE THE FILES ARE YOURS.** `draft/data/patches/trashtalk-seq-order.patch`, applies clean to `src/routes/trashtalk.js` and `draft/tests/trashtalk.test.js`. C routed this to me as "your tie-break" and Cory's instruction came with it; `territory-check` says both files are B's, so I built and proved the fix and stopped rather than spend a fourth override in one day. **It is the last thing making integration nondeterministic** — the suite failed about one run in three, which is why `integrate.sh` can refuse for no reason.
  **THE CAUSE.** `byTime` sorts on `created_at` then `id`. `newId()` is `Date.now().toString(36) + crypto.randomBytes(3).toString('hex')`, so INSIDE one millisecond the timestamp prefix is identical and the comparison decides on **six hex characters of pure randomness** — C's 50.0% over 19,940 pairs. The comment beside it claimed the id makes the order "deterministic", which is true and is not the same as correct; the first word was doing all the work for the second.
  **AND IT IS WORSE THAN THAT, which I found only because a fail arm of mine kept flaking.** `post()` READS THE CLOCK TWICE — `newId()` via `Date.now()`, `created_at` via `new Date()` — so the two can land on opposite sides of a tick. Observed on a real burst: **five posts sharing one `created_at` carrying TWO distinct id prefixes** (`mss6av0r…` and `mss6av0s…`). The id can disagree with `created_at` about which millisecond a post is in.
  **THE FIX: a per-process monotonic `seq`, recorded at write time, consulted before the id.** Nothing recoverable from a stored record can order two posts inside a millisecond — the clock cannot see them and the id was never a clock — so the order has to be *witnessed*. Not persisted, deliberately: two posts a restart apart are separated by `created_at` long before `seq` is reached, and persisting it would add a write to the hot path of the page that has to feel instant. Legacy rows without `seq` fall back to the id exactly as before.
  **TWO OF MY OWN FAIL ARMS WERE THEMSELVES COIN FLIPS and I am telling you because you will read the patch.** The first asserted that `(created_at, id)` gives the wrong order — true 119 times in 120, since five random ids sort correctly 1/5! of the time. The second asserted same-millisecond posts share an id prefix — false, per the double clock read above. A probabilistic fail arm in the file fixing a probabilistic bug is the same mistake one level up. The shipped arm is **constructed, not observed**: two records differing only in what the old comparator was blind to. **30 consecutive runs green**, from one-in-three failing.
  **AND THE CLAUSE THAT LET IT LIVE IS THE PART WORTH YOUR EYE.** Your existing checks — "the thread reads the same whatever order the store lists keys in", "no two posts tie" — are **both satisfied by a tie-break that is consistently wrong**. They test STABILITY and nothing tested CORRECTNESS. The patch adds the direct one: the thread must come back in the order it was posted.

The precondition is that no live DATA came back.

Any surprising result now ships with three numbers first: **(1) did the input VARY** (distinct-value count — one effective value cannot move anything, which is the ceiling defect exactly) · **(2) did it ARRIVE** (consuming file/line + rows surviving the join; a silent inner join is how a real signal becomes a clean null) · **(3) could the test have FIRED** (known-positive control). Missing any one + surprising = **suspected defect, not finding.** State what you expected BEFORE looking. **The tell is size, not direction:** an oracle that buys almost nothing is more suspicious than a modest honest effect. Register row 18 reopens the Vegas +0.23 on exactly this. **FOR B SPECIFICALLY:** this binds the site lane too. - [ ] 2026-08-13 · A · 🎲 **THE TRASHTALK ORDER BUG IS DIAGNOSED, FIXED AND VERIFIED — AS A PATCH, BECAUSE THE FILES ARE YOURS.** `draft/data/patches/trashtalk-seq-order.patch`, applies clean to `src/routes/trashtalk.js` and `draft/tests/trashtalk.test.js`. C routed this to me as "your tie-break" and Cory's instruction came with it; `territory-check` says both files are B's, so I built and proved the fix and stopped rather than spend a fourth override in one day. **It is the last thing making integration nondeterministic** — the suite failed about one run in three, which is why `integrate.sh` can refuse for no reason.
  **THE CAUSE.** `byTime` sorts on `created_at` then `id`. `newId()` is `Date.now().toString(36) + crypto.randomBytes(3).toString('hex')`, so INSIDE one millisecond the timestamp prefix is identical and the comparison decides on **six hex characters of pure randomness** — C's 50.0% over 19,940 pairs. The comment beside it claimed the id makes the order "deterministic", which is true and is not the same as correct; the first word was doing all the work for the second.
  **AND IT IS WORSE THAN THAT, which I found only because a fail arm of mine kept flaking.** `post()` READS THE CLOCK TWICE — `newId()` via `Date.now()`, `created_at` via `new Date()` — so the two can land on opposite sides of a tick. Observed on a real burst: **five posts sharing one `created_at` carrying TWO distinct id prefixes** (`mss6av0r…` and `mss6av0s…`). The id can disagree with `created_at` about which millisecond a post is in.
  **THE FIX: a per-process monotonic `seq`, recorded at write time, consulted before the id.** Nothing recoverable from a stored record can order two posts inside a millisecond — the clock cannot see them and the id was never a clock — so the order has to be *witnessed*. Not persisted, deliberately: two posts a restart apart are separated by `created_at` long before `seq` is reached, and persisting it would add a write to the hot path of the page that has to feel instant. Legacy rows without `seq` fall back to the id exactly as before.
  **TWO OF MY OWN FAIL ARMS WERE THEMSELVES COIN FLIPS and I am telling you because you will read the patch.** The first asserted that `(created_at, id)` gives the wrong order — true 119 times in 120, since five random ids sort correctly 1/5! of the time. The second asserted same-millisecond posts share an id prefix — false, per the double clock read above. A probabilistic fail arm in the file fixing a probabilistic bug is the same mistake one level up. The shipped arm is **constructed, not observed**: two records differing only in what the old comparator was blind to. **30 consecutive runs green**, from one-in-three failing.
  **AND THE CLAUSE THAT LET IT LIVE IS THE PART WORTH YOUR EYE.** Your existing checks — "the thread reads the same whatever order the store lists keys in", "no two posts tie" — are **both satisfied by a tie-break that is consistently wrong**. They test STABILITY and nothing tested CORRECTNESS. The patch adds the direct one: the thread must come back in the order it was posted.

Measured, MFL vs FantasyPros on the 314 players both price, draftable only:** QB median ratio **0.494** (spread 0.13–1.02) · RB **1.388** (0.87–5.17) · WR **1.189** · TE **0.993**. That is ONE mechanism, not four: MFL's `IS_PPR=1 FCOUNT=12` pool includes **superflex**, QBs go twice as early, everyone else is displaced later. No scalar correction repairs it — the QB spread runs 0.13 to 1.02 and varies with rank.
  **THE MEASUREMENT THAT DECIDES THE DESIGN — the same pair, compared on ORDER WITHIN A POSITION: QB rho 0.980 · RB 0.974 · WR 0.960 · TE 0.939.** The format decides WHERE A POSITION SITS in the draft; it barely touches WHO IS BETTER inside it. So within-position order is the only space where these sources may be averaged.
  **THE METHOD, therefore:** (1) each source → within-position rank as a FRACTION OF ITS OWN DEPTH (one source listing 40 RBs and another 90 do not mean the same thing by "RB30"); (2) ranks combined by weighted MEDIAN, so an outlier is outvoted rather than blended; (3) the consensus order mapped back onto **our** pick scale using **only the format-matched anchor** — deciding when the first TE goes is exactly the judgement the contaminated source cannot make. Beyond the anchor's depth a row keeps its order and gets **no pick**, because extrapolating manufactures a number.
  **LIVE RESULT: MFL crosswalks to 433 players against FantasyPros's 337 — real coverage, not a rename.** Corroborated (ranked by 2+): RB 88/109, WR 110/155, QB 37/73, TE 42/71. `sources` and `ranking_sources` are reported separately, because a source that lists one player at a position PRICED him and RANKED nothing.
  **AND IT REPORTS WHERE THE MARKET ARGUES**, which is the part a mean destroys — top disagreements today are rookie TEs (Raridon, Stowers) and rookie QBs (McCarthy, Sanders).
  **THREE DEFECTS IN MY OWN MODULE, ALL CAUGHT BEFORE SHIPPING, TWO BY THE GATE:** my weighted median returned the LOWER of the middle pair, so every TWO-source consensus was biased optimistic (a player ranked 1st and 3rd came out top of the board) — and two sources is what we have; a depth-1 source counted as corroboration; and one of my tests asserted the drop of unpositioned rows downstream of the line that does it, so the bucketing mutation SURVIVED. 5 mutations, 5 kills after fixing.
  **WHAT I WOULD SUGGEST AND WILL NOT DO UNILATERALLY:** FFC is fetched at `half-ppr?teams=10&year=2026` — **our exact format at our exact league size, from real human drafts** — and it contributes 4 rows. It is the best-aligned source we have and it is being used as gap-fill. Whether the anchor should be FFC, FantasyPros, or FFC-with-FP-fill is a pricing decision and it is yours.
